# ROADMAP — from here to a finished score

The phases and gates are defined in
[docs/plan-2026-09-22-from-here-to-a-finished-score.md](docs/plan-2026-09-22-from-here-to-a-finished-score.md).
This file is the STATUS of each item and nothing else. A session that takes
an item puts its branch beside it; a session that finishes one changes its
status and names the commit. Nobody writes a handoff — the line here and the
benchmark `FINDINGS.md` are the record.

Status words: `todo` · `in flight (branch)` · `done (sha)` · `blocked (why)`
· `dropped (why, DECISIONS line)`.

**Definition of done for the product** (plan §4): one command, a whole
movement, MusicXML + LilyPond PDF, unread bars marked never invented, every
staff named or held out, and a cleanup count Sean would rather pay than
re-enter.

---

A cleaning run, no behaviour change: `docs/audit-2026-10-07-code-cleaning.md` is the report and the
checklist of what is left. ROADMAP 0.4d done (`check` 247 → 192). CLAUDE.md §4c no longer types the
adjudicator list (51 in the tree, the text said 28) and §5a says a cloud clone is shallow. **For Sean,
before any public deployment of the web app:** scores have no owner column, `/uploads` is served without

and the JWT secret has a working default (report §3A). Nothing in GATHER/ADJUDICATE moved.

`version_memory.md` un-frozen (DECISIONS 2026-10-07) and brought current; the slow-tier text rule fixed (a measured
duration decides; comments/docstrings do not count; 39 files move to the fast tier); the 45 ignored-but-tracked
files resolved (1 untracked, 44 negated as raw data); remote branches HALF pruned: 67 stale unmerged copied to

   two notes on the same beat.

### How to work (all Sean, DECISIONS 2026-09-28/29)
- **Wiring and structure, proved by minimal tests** (one RED→GREEN per
  connection + one control) from a few real rows. No gathers, re-adjudications
  or crop batches per lane. **Machine time is free; context and tokens are
  not** — long runs are unattended nohup scripts that write a small summary.
- **Biggest difference first:** every lane names the funnel bucket it moves;
  what moves no top bucket is PARKED (2.28 page text, pedal/ottava output,
  bowing research).
- **Lanes run on Sonnet** (`model: "sonnet"`), max two at a time.
- **CLAUDE.md rule 7, new line: before trusting any result, ask who says it's
  right** — Sean, the print or the reference encoding is evidence; a model's
  own output, or a result whose raw data is gone, is not.
- **New weights are tested Sean's way:** graft into a COPY of production and
  compare the two on GATHER+ADJUDICATE only — `python3 -m tools.omr.staged
  … --through adjudicate`; harness `benchmarks/omr-weights-ab-2026-09/run_ab.sh`
  (P-vs-P control = zero differences). The graft tool

  prints a SELF-CHECK on every build (refuses if anything but the grafted rows

The content cannot shrink (`basis` feeds `Log.closure` and the circularity filter), so the fix is SPELLING, in the file only: `tools/omr/staged/record_io.py` pools lists that repeat within (quantity, decider, system) as one core under `record.pools` plus each verdict's private ids at their positions (`{"$pool": id, "ins": [[0, "obs:…"]]}`), interns repeated `correlated` groups by content, and `load_record` expands a file back to exactly the dict `Log.to_json` produced. `Log`/`Verdict`/`Evidence`/every stage untouched; the in-memory `result` the CLI hands `--musicxml` is never pooled; a list under 64 ids or a group of one is written inline, so small fixtures are byte-identical. **Measured** (`probe/pool_control.py`, compact → pooled): Litolff **75.0 → 32.2 MB, 8.0 MB/page**; Breitkopf **237.3 → 52.7 MB, 13.2 MB/page** — both under budget; 22 % / 11 % of the original `indent=2` files. (2) `Evidence.correlated_groups()` did an O(n^2) all-pairs walk over everything a decision consulted (`_seen`, ~770 rows for every one of `arc_owner`'s 779 arcs) plus an unmemoised `Log.closure()` BFS per pair -- measured at 26x the rest of the decision (8.6s vs 0.34s / 64 arcs); now a bucket-by-(reader,frame,quantity) + union-find pass, `closure()` memoised (a pure function of an immutable `basis`, so nothing to invalidate). **Combined, unprofiled: ADJUDICATE 29.9s / GROUPS 1.9s / EVALUATE 13.4s / INFER 0.3s on the 4-page record** (was: did not finish `arc_owner` within ~100s of a killed profiling run; a base-vs-arm control over the same 13-decision/one-system subset ran the UNPATCHED code to 468 CPU-seconds and still had not finished, against the fix's 16.461s -- a measured **28x floor**). Swept over all sixteen, **2.4a would refuse 1 of 16 (6%)** — so the 'reach is an upper bound' caveat is correct but SMALL, and the pre-2.4a record does not inflate reach materially. Sean answered: all 8 crops are ONE voice, by three rules — (1) same beat, opposite stems, is ALWAYS two voices; (2) two streams NOT lined up are two voices where EACH sums to a full measure on its own AND they sit in separate, vertically non-overlapping bands on the staff; (3) a single line summing to one measure is one voice; neither fits → abstain (rule 8). `adjudicate_voices` gates the legacy split's own candidate on all three, in that order (rule 3 tried before rule 2 as the simpler, more committal claim). Reuses rule 1 unchanged from the withdrawn `claude/voice-split-2.21` (same-beat via `Q.ONSET_COLUMN`, page-x fallback); rules 2/3 are new — `_meter_in_force` reads `Q.METER` (`voted` or `carried`, resolved to the right segment with `meter_at`), `_sum_beats` sums `Q.DURATION.beats` per event, `_vertically_separated` compares the RANGE (not the mean) of `Q.NOTEHEAD_STAFF_POSITION`. `adjudicate.ORDER`: `Q.METER` and `Q.ONSET_COLUMN` both moved before `Q.VOICES` — and `Q.EVENT` before `Q.METER`, since `Q.METER` itself wants `Q.EVENT`, a real dependency `inventory --check` caught on the first attempt (`Q.METER` placed ahead of `Q.EVENT`) in one line. Per CLAUDE.md 2026-09-29 ("our process spends too much time and context on pricing runs"), proved with MICROSCOPIC RED→GREEN tests only, no re-gather: `test_staged_voices.py`'s `TestTheVoiceSplit` grew to 11 tests covering the manager's five scenarios (a flipping line summing to the bar → `one_voice_sums`; same beat, opposite stems, no meter injected at all → `two_voices_same_beat`; two offset full streams, upper above lower → `two_voices_separated_and_full`; two offset full streams interleaved in height, Sean's own control → ABSTAIN; neither sums → ABSTAIN) plus a `no_meter` abstain test. RED confirmed by reverting both production files to `origin/main` with the new tests in place: 7 of 7 new/changed tests failed, the other 14 (unrelated mechanisms) stayed green. `pytest -m "not slow"`: main's count + 7 net new, 0 failed. `staged.check` TOTAL **247, unchanged**; `inventory --check` 10 open, 0 not on `KNOWN_GAPS`. | 3.1 | LilyPond exporter for staged, reusing the `_lily_*` renderers | **done** (`claude/staged-lilypond-3.1` @ `bdd3896f`, off `origin/main` `ea4e45a9`). `tools/omr/staged/lilypond.py:to_lilypond` calls `export.build()`/`_pair_arcs`/`_place_wedges`/`_events`/`_voice_split` over a fresh `Record` — the SAME functions `to_musicxml` calls — then renders with the legacy exporter's own PURE renderers (`_lily_event`, `_lily_measure`, `_lily_measure_rest`, `_lily_key_name`, `_clef_to_lily`, `_lily_wedge_plan`); `tools/omr/export.py` untouched (`git diff` empty). ⚠️ ONE STRUCTURAL DEPARTURE FROM LEGACY, NAMED IN THE MODULE DOCSTRING: legacy's own `to_lilypond` emits one `\new Staff` per (page, system, staff), so a hairpin or slur crossing a system break is dropped by construction; this emits one `\new Staff` per PART (spanning every system, matching MusicXML's `<part>`) and gets the cross-system merge for free from the same `_pair_arcs`/`_place_wedges` passes, so that is NOT a documented drop here. Returns `(str, dict)`, not the bare `str` the roadmap line named — deviation stated up front, for the same reason `to_musicxml` returns a report: a documented drop must be counted, not merely true. **Measured on all three acceptance documents** (`benchmarks/omr-staged-lilypond-2026-09/compare_notes.py`): engraved Beethoven 5 mvt1 bars 1-24 (18 parts, 358 notes), Litolff Beethoven 5 mvt1 pp.1-4 scan (12 parts, 965 notes), Breitkopf Brahms 1 mvt1 pp.0-3 scan (14 parts, 2513 notes) — **44 of 44 parts across the three documents agree on their sorted pitch multiset with the MusicXML export of the same record; all three compile with `lilypond` at exit 0, 0 errors** (~1 s / ~4.7 s / ~8.8 s). Bar-check warnings (17 / 137 / 550) trace to a PRE-EXISTING bar-sum shortfall already present in the MusicXML file (confirmed by summing `<duration>` per bar independently), not to this exporter. "Unterminated tie" warnings (0 / 77 / 400) are LilyPond's pitch-matching tie resolution independently corroborating the tie-pairing defect CLAUDE.md's `omr-chord-tie-2026-09` already measured — a third instrument on a known finding, found by accident of compiling. ⚠️ ONE BUG FOUND AND FIXED measuring the engraved record: a fermata over a whole-bar measure rest was silently dropped (`_lily_measure_rest` builds a bare rest from the meter alone) — `_Bar.fermata` + `_render_bar` now append `\fermata`, restoring exact parity (77 = 77). ⚠️ ONE LIMITATION FOUND AND LEFT, because fixing it would be porting rather than reusing: `_lily_event`'s chord branch writes ONE articulation suffix per chord from `group_chords_in_measure`'s event-level DEDUPLICATED list, where `_mxl_note` writes one per notehead — a chord whose members share a mark collapses (Breitkopf: 112 MusicXML marks → 106 written), counted in `report["lilypond"]["articulation_marks_collapsed_per_chord"]`, zero on the other two documents. `--lilypond` wired into both `staged/__main__.py` and `staged/export.py`'s own CLI beside `--musicxml`, imported after the gather for the same reason; `reach.STAGE_OF_FILE` registers `lilypond.py` under `EXPORT` (it names no `Q.*` quantity itself). `tools/omr/tests/test_staged_lilypond.py` (17 tests, run RED against the unbuilt module first) reuses `test_staged_export.py`'s fixture helpers rather than duplicating them. `pytest -m "not slow"`: 2759 passed (up from 2731 on `ea4e45a9`, no regressions). `python3 -m tools.omr.staged.check`: 252 open findings, unchanged from `origin/main` alone — this lane adds zero. ⚠️ **What is NOT established**: exact per-VOICE note ORDER on the three real documents (the acceptance check compares sorted pitch multisets per part; order-exact agreement is checked only on the small synthetic fixtures in the test file, because reconstructing it reliably from real chord/tuplet-bearing LilyPond text via regex on 965-2513-note documents was judged not worth building this pass); no print was consulted; no OMR-NED (musicdiff scores MusicXML, not this exporter). `tools/omr/acceptance.py`'s roadmap-1.3 `musicxml2ly` stopgap was deliberately NOT rewired in this lane per the coordinator's instruction — the swap (call `to_lilypond` directly on the same in-memory record, drop the `musicxml2ly` round-trip, fold `report["lilypond"]` into `current.json`) is described for whoever schedules it. |

Part A (the product definition, CLAUDE.md §1: "one command, a whole movement, MusicXML and a LilyPond PDF"): `--lilypond` already shipped (roadmap 3.1); this lane adds `--pdf` to `tools/omr/staged/__main__.py`. It compiles the `.ly` with the `lilypond` binary, located the same way `tools.omr.acceptance.lilypond_check` does since 3.1b (`shutil.which("lilypond")`, never a hardcoded path); reuses `--lilypond`'s already-computed text when both are given (one `to_lilypond` call, not two); writes a `.ly` anyway, beside the PDF, when `--lilypond` was not given; and reports bar-check failures / unterminated ties parsed from lilypond's own stderr with the SAME two regexes `tools.omr.acceptance` uses (deferred-imported, never re-derived, so the two call sites can never read one stderr string differently). `lilypond` absent, or a compile that exceeds a 1800s timeout, still writes the `.ly` and exits 0 with a clear message — CLAUDE.md rule 8, never a crash. A new `_print_accounting_summary` prints `unread bars` / `staves held out` in plain words at the very end of a `--musicxml`/`--lilypond` run, reading `bars_held_out_sum` / `part_join.held_out_staves` straight off the exporter's own coverage report — never recomputed, and it says "not computed" rather than a bare 0 when `--musicxml` was not given (CLAUDE.md rule 8 again). CLAUDE.md §3's "does not yet export LilyPond" line and §5b's option list (missing `--lilypond`/`--pdf`) are fixed; nothing else in CLAUDE.md touched.

Part B (page cap → job budget): `tools/omr/staged/budget.py` (new — see roadmap 1.2b, closed by this same branch) is the one place the per-page time constants live. `backend/core/config.py` gains `omr_job_budget_s` (default 50400s / ~14h — chosen so the one measured 16-page whole-movement run, ~12.7–13.9h depending on the direction-text gate, fits under it with headroom; override `OMR_JOB_BUDGET_S`). `backend/main.py`'s `POST /api/scores/{id}/process/omr` accepts an optional `pages` query param (staged only — any other engine gets a 400 if it sends one); when given, it is parsed with `tools.omr.staged.__main__.parse_pages` (imported via new `staged_omr.parse_page_range`, never a second web-only spelling), priced with `staged_omr.estimate_budget_s` **before** `score.status` is set to `"processing"`, and refused with **413** (the upper-bound estimate, the expected estimate, the data point and `OMR_JOB_BUDGET_S` itself all named in the error) when the conservative UPPER BOUND exceeds the limit — never the smaller "expected" figure, and never a silent truncation to `OMR_MAX_PAGES`. `pages=None` (no query param — every existing caller) is BYTE-IDENTICAL to before: `backend/modules/staged_omr.py`'s `_run_staged_blocking` only overrides `OMR_MAX_PAGES` when an explicit page list is passed in, proved by a test that monkeypatches `run_staged_omr` and asserts it was called with `pages=None`. The budget dict and the resolved page list are stored on `Score.metadata_json` (`omr_budget`, `omr_pages_requested`) at both job start and completion, not just logged. Frontend: `ScoreProcess.tsx`'s "Staged (experimental)" selector grows an optional page-range text input (blank = the old cap); `runOMR` in `api/client.ts` grows an optional fourth `pages` argument. Containers were NOT rebuilt (per the brief — the running app may be in use).

**Verified end to end, real weights, one page, `--no-surya --no-ocr`** (`benchmarks/omr-staged-engraved-2026-09/out/fixture/beethoven-sym5-mvt1-m1-24.pdf` page 0, `--dpi 300`, `deepscoresv2-yolov8l-imgsz2048-ft-30ep.pt`, symlinked weights/library per §5a): one command wrote a 47,207-byte MusicXML (18 parts), a 4,256-byte `.ly` and a 126,699-byte PDF; `lilypond exit 0`, **0 bar-check failures, 0 unterminated ties**; the printed accounting line read **"0 unread bars of 126 bars carrying events"** and **"staves held out: 0"**.

The job-budget refusal prices the REQUESTED page count from the query string, not the PDF's actual page count (unknown at that point without opening the file) — a request for more pages than the document has is priced, and likely refused, as if they all existed, which is the conservative direction. |

Both are now separate `StagedOmrResult` fields, each off `staged.export.to_musicxml`'s own `report["unread_bar_marks"]` (never recomputed): `unread_bars` (`["unread"]`), `held_out_bars` (`["held_out_sum"]`), plus `bars_total` (`unread_bars + bars_with_events`, the one arithmetic this module performs, summing two already-computed exporter counts for display) and `held_out_bars_by_page` (grouping roadmap 2.8's own per-bar `held` list by page — never a second count). `backend/main.py` writes all four onto `Score.metadata_json` as `staged_unread_bars` / `staged_held_out_bars` / `staged_bars_total` / `staged_held_out_bars_by_page`, beside the existing `staged_held_out_staves` / `staged_status_census`; a `local`/`claude_vision` job carries none of them. The marked PDF link needed NO new backend route: `GET /api/scores/{id}/export?format=pdf` already prefers the native staged exporter for a staged score (roadmap 3.1/3.3) and already marks unread/held-out bars in red with a reason word (roadmap 3.5) — generated on first request, exactly as the brief anticipated. Frontend: `frontend/src/components/StagedAccountingPanel.tsx` (new) reads the four metadata fields straight off `Score.metadata_json`, renders nothing for a non-staged job or before EXPORT has run (no zeroed placeholder), and renders "N of M bars unread · K held (do not add up) · S staves held out" plus an "Open marked PDF" button that reuses the existing `exportScore(id, 'pdf')` blob-download flow (`Export.tsx`'s own pattern) — no new API client function. Mounted on `ScoreProcess.tsx` (below the step indicator) and `ReviewUI.tsx` (below the header), since a staged job's `status` flips to `"review"` and `ScoreProcess.tsx` auto-navigates to `ReviewUI.tsx` before a user reliably sees its own "done" panel — the panel needed to live on the page the user actually lands on, not only the one named in the brief. Tests (RED→GREEN, confirmed by reverting the two source-file edits via `git apply -R` and rerunning): `backend/tests/test_staged_accounting_3_3c.py` (3 tests — a two-bar fixture, one held-out-by-sum, one truly unread, `staged_unread_bars`/`staged_held_out_bars`/`staged_bars_total`/`staged_held_out_bars_by_page` correct and separate; a `local` job's POSITIVE CONTROL carries zero `staged_*` keys; the PDF link resolves through the REAL route, REAL `export_module` dispatch and the REAL `lilypond` binary — not mocked — asserting `%PDF` magic bytes). `pytest backend/` (system python3.9, no docker): 120 passed (117 baseline + 3 new), same 2 pre-existing `test_export_module.py` failures the brief named, unchanged. `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: 73 errors before this branch's frontend changes, 73 after (measured both ways in the same session by reverting/reapplying the two page edits) — zero added. **One look**: rendered via a throwaway `frontend/harness.html` + `src/harness-main.tsx` (deleted before commit) mounting the panel with mock staged/local scores, served by a manually-started `vite --port 5199` (the Browser pane's own `.claude/launch.json` resolves to a DIFFERENT, pre-existing worktree than this one, so `preview_start` could not be pointed at this branch's own config — noted for whoever next needs a per-worktree frontend preview) — screenshot confirmed "37 of 184 bars unread · 12 held (do not add up) · 2 staves held out · Open marked PDF →" for the staged mock and nothing at all for the local mock. Fence respected: `tools/omr/` untouched (`git diff --stat -- tools/omr` empty). |

A human box lacks every raster-derived mark (`Q.GLYPH_CONF`, band distance, ladder, beam, flag, dot) — ⚠️ so its duration is read from the head alone (a quarter), which is wrong for every beamed note and nothing on the row says so; pinned. What reached nothing, by kind, and why — the list this page exists to produce: **18 of 23 `nothing` labels reached no stage** because the boxes were `ledgerLine` ×11, `arpeggiato` ×3, `accidentalFlat` ×2, `accidentalNatural`, `restQuarter` — only NOTEHEADS have an 'is this really one' refusal that reads a human witness; no other family has a precision decision at all (→ 3.4g); **2 of 6 `belongs to Violin II` reached nothing** because both were accidentals and ownership is a contest over noteheads only (→ 2.7's `accidental_owner`); the 3 confirms reach nothing by design; 9 of 11 drawn boxes were weighed (duration/event/stem/whole-rest/precision), the two not weighed being the clef box (→ 3.4f) and a flat (→ 2.7); 18 of 20 redraws weighed by duration/stem. Files under `out/sean-staff-12-1-2/` and `out/sean-staff-2-1-9/`.** ⚠️ **The 59 `redraw_box` actions on the two new staves are of UNCERTAIN intent** — Sean: *"I didn't resize the 2 new ones"*, then *"actually, maybe I did — I can't remember specifically"* — some or all may be drags the viewer filed on a click with a few pixels of mouse travel (median shift 0.06 space, 0 vertical, size unchanged; measured 2026-09-24). A viewer bug (→ 3.4h: a drag threshold, and the 59 marked accidental in the FINDINGS, not deleted from the sidecars). Sean's separate observation — *"the boxes were just a bit off geometrically — the answers were correct and they were very close but not exact"* — is his eye's and is NOT measured by those drags; it stays an open observation until a deliberate resize pass measures it. Sean also: *"a lot of uncolored boxes around partial staff lines — shouldn't those get erased?"* — they are `ledgerLine` boxes (3.4g/3.4g-2 refuse them; 2.14 makes the refusal reach the ladder) and `staff`-class boxes (CV-only, never a symbol); the viewer must hide the latter and show refusals as refused (3.4h). Design:  *"a UI where it pulls up a staff on a system and shows me everything it is gathering — I give feedback by redrawing, deleting or adding boxes — then the next stage, you tell me what the stage is doing with that information and I give feedback on what is working and what isn't — each stage at a time, following the trail of information and decisions with human feedback."* Purpose, his words: *"I mainly want this information so that we can then use it to determine how to refine the build, rules and decisions — structurally. All of the info would be given to you to turn into fixes."* Corrections are WITNESSES (observations with `reader: sean`, appended, never mutating a machine row) that the stages RE-DECIDE with; a disagreement with a verdict is filed against the verdict id, never applied. First slice on Litolff p3 `staff/3/0/9` (Viola). Two lanes on one contract, the review-actions sidecar (`tools/omr/staged/review/`): (A) `human_evidence.py` ingests the sidecar into a record via `record_io`, `rerun.py` re-runs ADJUDICATE→EXPORT off the saved record and diffs verdicts, `feedback.py` writes the session-readable feedback file (every human row, the verdicts whose basis names it, every disagreement with the verdict's reason and rows); (B) `server.py` + static: pick a staff-system ordered by heads lost, GATHER view on the 600-dpi crop (staff lines, cells, detector boxes by class, ink), add/delete/redraw → sidecar; ADJUDICATE / EVALUATE / INFER / EXPORT views per glyph with reason + rows used, agree/disagree → sidecar; a re-run button calls (A). Wired here: `_collect_bars` now tracks `in_force` (the LilyPond twin of `_part_xml`'s own variable) and calls `SX._bar_holds_out` with `SX._divisions(parts)` (the SAME ruler `to_musicxml` uses, never a second one) for every bar with events, before the lone-measure-rest special case (which can never itself be held out, so the order changes nothing else); a held bar becomes an "empty" `_Bar` with a `reason`, and `_render_bar` prepends `\once \override Rest.color = #red` (or `MultiMeasureRest.color`, read off the token LilyPond actually returned) plus `^\markup { "..." }`. Rendered page 2 (`benchmarks/acceptance/out/print/marked-bars-3.5.png`, `pdftoppm -r 80`): every held-out bar shows a short red rest with "held: does not add up" printed above its own staff, legible and not overlapping. ⚠️ Worth flagging, not fixed here (out of this lane's fence — 2.12h, the opening-meter lane, and rhythm.py generally, are the natural owners): the whole-movement Brahms compile logs 323 `barcheck failed` warnings and 183 `unterminated tie` warnings — smaller than the near-total cascade a fully-unmarked run would produce (2.8 already holds 4,513 of 5,792 bars-with-events out), and the SAME class roadmap 3.1 already named on the small Breitkopf fixture ("a PRE-EXISTING bar-sum shortfall already present in the MusicXML file, not to this exporter") — but not independently re-confirmed as the same cause at whole-movement scale, so stated as measured fact rather than diagnosis. Also observed, not measured: on a densely mixed page (marked and unmarked bars interleaved, e.g. compiled page 6), LilyPond's automatic spacing can pack a `^\markup` word close enough to a neighbouring bar's notehead to be visually tight — the uniformly-marked page committed here shows no such crowding, but a page mixing many short and long bars might; nothing in this brief's proof budget covers a spacing fix. |

Lane detail: `claude/import-4.1` `18961007`. `tools/reengrave/__main__.py` (CLI), `planning.py` (pure: ranking, `--pages`/`--movements` resolution, the staged command), `fetch.py` (the two side-effecting seams: opening the browser, watching the download directory). `planning.rank_editions` reuses `tools.library.build_wishlist`'s own `score_candidate`/`familiar_publishers` over held catalog editions AND catalogued-but-not-held `data/score-library/wishlist.json` rows (deduplicated by imslp id), never restating the scoring; a live IMSLP fetch is attempted only when NEITHER source has anything. A held edition is used directly with the browser never touched (CLAUDE.md §8's hard constraint); a not-held one opens the IMSLP page (`open`/`webbrowser.open`, never the gated download itself), watches `--downloads-dir` (default `~/Downloads`) for a new PDF against a pre-open snapshot so a stale file already there cannot be mistaken for it, times out with a clear message, then hands the file to `tools.library.ingest.ingest_imslp` and reads the resulting path off a freshly rebuilt catalog (`ingest_imslp` itself returns no path). `--movements` passes straight through unchanged to the staged CLI; omitted, the whole work runs as ONE movement over its full known page range, printed loudly (rule 6: never guessed). ## Phase 5 — Clean (ongoing)

Cleanup count every two weeks on the fixed pages; corrections filed as
evidence; auto-accept rules from recorded verdicts. Done for a publisher when
Sean would rather fix than re-enter.
